# SkipWait decision register (single source of truth)

## Owner direction - FIFO queue order (October 31, 2026, 11:54 IST)

At 11:53 IST the owner instructed "No go by priority queue only", then at 11:54 IST clarified "FIFO always". Oldest queued work first, not opportunistic coordinator selection or an automatic new-feature jump. Keep both Claude seats and other sessions productive within that order, existing role eligibility, ownership and holds. Inspect actual unfinished acceptance; report a picker/order conflict through Instinct before the affected dispatch, without silently overriding gates or taking another holder's work. Preserve already-started work during a routing correction. No product, spending, production, migration, settings, ownership, review or CI change is granted. Sources: `phonemsg-01M68A1QZGARZGJNSVWG9NEBZY`, `phonemsg-01M68A28FYPYGJSQ7X9FBV64JS`. See `AGENTS.md` and `docs/BUILD_CONTINUITY.md`. This dated source record is not a numbered-register collision or a claim of picker enforcement/session acknowledgment.


Every owner decision lives here. Any doc, code, copy, test or config that disagrees with an **active** entry is wrong and must be fixed (or the doc marked "Superseded by D-xxx"). Read this with `AGENTS.md` section 0 and `docs/KIT_V4_BUILD_PLAN.md`.

Reconciliation: this newer owner instruction supersedes D-009's discretionary P0 / in-progress / lowest-unblocked selection order wherever it conflicts with FIFO. D-010's goal remains; current authorization, dependencies, owner holds, role eligibility, file ownership and independent-review/CI gates remain. This is not permission to seize a held lane or to implement work for which a required grant is absent. Record the concrete order/eligibility conflict through Instinct rather than inventing an exception or asking the founder to repeat FIFO.

This adds no spending, posts-in-the-owner's-name, production/security change or review/CI waiver; a genuinely user-only approval still goes to the owner.

Group Plan correction (October 31, 11:58 IST): the owner recalled the frozen group plan and clarified "Not institute" (`phonemsg-01M68AAGS240E9PE6F3KMQ2PZT`, `phonemsg-01M68AAR5CW1S2GBJQNRF42T6M`). No new price catalog, live switch, implementation ownership or FIFO override is granted.

Group seat clarification (October 31, 11:59 IST): "Groups can buy a certain number of seats or can increase 1 by 1 post a group buy" (`phonemsg-01M68ACCV7ASBCRVTDF80QXS5S`). Support initial seat quantity plus later one-seat additions to the existing group purchase, not another compulsory bulk block. Existing candidate identity, payment verification, activation and idempotency requirements remain. Proration, renewal alignment and discount eligibility for a later single-seat addition remain unverified commercial details; use frozen rules/current purchase terms before proposing a choice, with no invented price or broadening of the original 10% scope. No FIFO override or implementation/live-effect grant is added.

## How decisions are made (binding, owner Oct 14 2026)
**Every platform and agent (Claude, Codex, Gemini, Copilot, Instinct, Devin, humans):** before merging any change that alters product behaviour, auth, money, data or copy, check it against this register; if it embodies a new decision, add the entry (or ask the owner) in the same PR.
1. **Audit first.** Inspect the project area and the evidence before proposing anything.
2. **Propose with evidence.** Each proposal states: the problem; data (production metrics/analytics, with the query or source), research (cited URL, publisher, year), behavioural psychology (the seeker's and the referrer's mental model at that step and the principle used), proven patterns from comparable products; the metric it moves and the expected effect; cost, risk and alternatives; and how it will be measured after release.
3. **Impact check.** List every older decision (by ID) and every doc, code area, copy, test and config the proposal touches or contradicts. Never silently override an older decision — the owner chooses which wins.
4. **Owner approves manually.** Ask targeted questions whenever intent, scope or a trade-off is unclear. No new change, redesign, pricing or process change is coded before explicit approval. Exceptions: P0 incidents (fix, then report) and tasks already approved in the queue.
5. **Record, then sync the whole project.** Add the entry here first (docs first), then update every impacted doc/code/copy/test in the same change set or as queued tasks. An entry is `synced` only when nothing in the repo contradicts it.
6. **Measure.** After release, record before/after numbers against the metric named in the entry.

Ethics guardrail for every psychology-based proposal: no dark patterns, false urgency, fabricated proof or scarcity, guilt-tripping, or pressure on referrers; referring always stays voluntary; real numbers only.

No implementation, sales enablement or founder resolution of the packet's open questions is claimed. This dated source record preserves the instruction immediately without inventing a conflicting numbered decision; normal register integration must check all open branches before assigning the next D-ID.

| D-035 | 2026-10-16 | **REJECTED by founder Ayu, Oct 23 (D-147) (attribution unverified - no recovered owner original; register state and refund behavior unchanged); historical proposal below, not implementation authority.** **A referrer decline shows a one-tap anonymous reason, and the reason decides the credit.** Reasons are one tap (optional note), split into *fixable* (profile incomplete, skill not shown, note unclear, level mismatch) and *not fixable* (role closed, can only refer people I know, wrong team). The seeker sees "A verified employee at <Company> said: <reason>", never the referrer's name. A not-fixable reason releases the credit; a fixable reason keeps it spent, as payment for the review. After fixing the flagged item the seeker may "Send again" for 1 credit, once per job, and only when the flagged item actually changed. The resend goes to colleagues, and to the same referrer only if they opted in. D-021 is unchanged: the declined ask still reopens to colleagues. Measure: decline-with-reason rate, fix rate, resend vs first-send acceptance, decline vs ignore rate before and after. | Owner's own proposal Oct 16 ("show the rejected with reason to correct in users profile and again they can send once corrected. again costs another credit"), approved with the guardrails above. Rivals refund undelivered referrals (RefOpen holds the charge "until a referral is actually made"; FindMyReferral escrow; seen Oct 16). Pay-per-attempt analogs draw the worst sentiment (Thumbtack 2.1/5 on Trustpilot). A charge reads as fair when its cost is visible (Kahneman, Knetsch & Thaler 1986). Loss aversion λ ≈ 1.96 (Brown et al., JEL 2024). | Supersedes D-012(2) and D-021 "the credit stays spent" for not-fixable declines; keeps them for fixable ones. | D-076 | 2026-10-18 | **Rulings inside the approved money rules, so no implementer has to guess (decided under D-074; each changeable by the owner).** (1) **"Released" credit** (D-035 not-fixable pass, D-038 lapses, D-066 step back): the pickup that consumed the credit is undone. The ask reopens with its one credit still attached and unspent. The credit comes back once if the reopened ask is withdrawn or expires unclaimed, and only the next pickup spends it again. No ledger row moves at release. The seeker is never charged twice, and an ask is never delivered free. Refund eligibility becomes "no claim since the latest release". (2) **Claim lapse (D-038a):** 48 h from the claim, with a reminder at 24 h (in-app now, email with D-060). The lapse is a recorded transition: it clears the referrer, releases their slot, excludes that referrer from the ask for good and tells the seeker plainly. It is not a pass: no reason, no "passed" notice. The 5-claim cap counts open claims only. (3) **Allocation lapse:** a delivered or queue-allocated ask that its referrer does not claim, accept or pass within 48 h of first visibility leaves that referrer the way a claim lapse does. It returns to the company's open queue for every available colleague, like a D-021 pass; no new credit is spent and that referrer is excluded. The 7-day expiry from first visibility still bounds the ask; the clock does not restart (as in D-021 and D-061). (4) **Referred deadline (D-038b):** 5 days from acceptance, reminders on day 2 and day 4, and one "need 3 more days" (the deadline becomes day 8). "Referred" is the referrer's mark or the seeker's "I got the referral email"; either one stops the clock and moves the ask to `intro_made`. A missed deadline, or D-066's "I can't refer this after all", reopens the ask to colleagues with the credit released as in (1), revokes the document grant and excludes that referrer. (5) **No retroactive storms:** for claims and acceptances that exist at deploy, the 48 h and 5-day clocks start at deploy time. (6) **Send again (D-035):** offered only after a fixable pass, once per seeker and job link, and only when, after the pass, the ask note was rewritten, the profile was updated or a new resume was uploaded (enforced on the server). It closes the reopened original without a refund (it was picked up and stays spent) and creates a new ask that spends 1 credit under the normal D-034 rule (D-066(1)). The new ask goes to colleagues, and to the passing referrer only if they ticked "send it back to me if they update it" when they passed (default off). (7) **Legacy passes:** a pass without one of the seven reason codes counts as not fixable, so the credit is released. (8) **What counts as a referral (D-066(3)):** an ask is referred when it reaches `intro_made` or later through the referrer's mark or the seeker's confirmation. Every public or wall "referral" number counts only those; "accepted" numbers say accepted. | D-085 | 2026-10-20 | **How the agents work together, and who does what — in effect now.** Owner, verbatim: "if i tell multiple agents to work (by giving commands resume,continue or build) multiple model agents should collaborate and take next task not take the same task and conflict. make our functioning well organised"; "if soemthing is half built and not completely built and no one is working on it. the next agent should work on the half built one instead of taking new one. so that the priority tasks are always built first over next. half built priority tasks are always handled first there should never be an incident priority from top tasks are pending but the lower priority ones are getting executed"; "always check the local and repo . make sure they are always in sync (if something is working and not green yet . it is not currently handled by anyone the next agent should handle)"; "you(claude) always work like ceo in the architecting high stakes decision making,direction, handling entire business to earn money as well as give value to users, re analysing what we are building to make our platform goto global platform for referrals, what to do , what we are doing , what can be improved, like a ceo . im using cto like open frontier models to develop"; "implementing the CEO/engineering-team role split should be immediate"; "work like a organisation, all open models (which are cto,cdo, and all other) should report about all green priority status to ceo(claude). You ceo(claude decide the direction and intelligence to reach our goal of to be the first website for everyone to get who ever searches for job referrals) it should satisfy users and earn money for us"; "every agent should know,in sync on our priorities top to down , whether green or not . top priorities should always be green before touching the next priority"; "completing a pending task is important than taking new one"; and "always make sure local and git repo are in sync". So: (1) `build`, `resume`, `continue` and `next` all mean one thing: run `node scripts/next-task.mjs --claim "<model name>"` and do only what it prints; nobody resumes or chooses work from memory. A name with no ` · <session>` part gets a unique session tag from the picker (for example `Qwen 3 · k3f9`), which it prints; the agent uses exactly that full name for `--heartbeat`, `--report`, `--fixed`, `--verdict` and on resume (read from the holder column of `--status`, never invented), and two sessions never share a name. (2) Your own unfinished work comes back first (a build, a review, findings to fix, a landing, a packet, the red-main fix), so a restarted agent never leaves a half-built claim idle. Except: pending work ranked above your own task with nobody on it (a review, findings to fix, a red pull request whose owner is quiet, a landing, a half-built claim quiet for 2 hours, an unblock for the CEO) is handed to you first; you push your own work first (the picker enforces it), and your own claim stays yours for 2 hours. (3) Then finishing comes before starting: one top-down pass over Claude Code's order for pending work nobody is on — a review, findings to fix, a pull request with red CI, a green pull request nobody lands, a half-built claim, a blocked task — and only then a second top-down pass for new work. A priority that is red (red CI, or its team reported red or blocked) holds everything below it for everyone else: no new or pending work below it starts; an agent already holding work below it whose owner is active keeps going on its own work (its hands cannot help the red one); a red priority with nobody on it is handed out first; new work ranked above it may still start, and a blocked task goes to the CEO to unblock. Red `main` still comes before everything. (4) "Nobody is on it" means no commit and no heartbeat (`--heartbeat --as`) pushed for 2 hours (queue rule 2 was 24 hours); agents push after every commit, heartbeat before long steps, and commit and push before they stop. A holder that comes back after 90 minutes or more re-takes its claim on the same branch a takeover would push to, so the first push wins and two agents never both hold it. A claim, takeover, review, fix or landing is a marker pushed to origin, so two agents racing for one task cannot both win. (5) Local and origin stay in sync: the picker reads the local clone, refuses new work while your own claimed branch has unpushed commits or uncommitted changes, and reports any other local-only work and a stale local `main`. (6) **Roles:** Claude Code is the CEO — direction, high-stakes decisions, architecture, the business (revenue and user value), the continuous re-analysis of what we build toward the go-to global referral platform, packets, Tier 1 reviews and the queue order; the open frontier models are the CTO and engineering team and build from packets. The picker enforces it: Claude Code always claims as `--claim "Claude Code"` (any name starting with "Claude" is the CEO) and is offered red `main`, its own unfinished work, unblocks of blocked priorities, packets, Tier 1 reviews and Tier 1 landings after a passed review, never a build, takeover, make-green or findings fix, and the CEO review when nothing at its level waits; below a red priority it only writes packets and unblocks. (7) **The organisation reports to the CEO:** the open models are the CTO, CDO and the rest of the team (the role follows the lane: design is the CDO's, everything else the CTO's); each reports every priority's status — green, red, blocked, in review, landed — with `--report` when it stops, lands, opens a pull request or gets blocked (a marker on `agent/reports/log`). Every agent and the CEO read one board (`--status`, and the top of every picker run): every priority top down, its health, holder, quiet time, pull request and latest report. The CEO decides direction toward the goal — the first site anyone uses when they look for a job referral — so users are satisfied and SkipWait earns money. (8) Exit codes: 0 work to do (claimed, continue, CEO review), 2 STOP (GitHub unreadable: main's CI, the pull-request list or a task pull request's CI), 3 SYNC FIRST (your own work is not on origin), 4 WAIT, 5 NOTHING TO PICK. (9) A squash-merged pull request counts as done even before its status is set to done. (10) One packet per session (D-074 (2) stays): after a Tier 1 draft pull request or a landing, the agent reports to the CEO and ends the session; the owner's next `build` starts a new session, which runs the picker. (11) **The CEO decides inside the owner's decisions** (owner, Oct 20, verbatim: "(claude) you do the thinking part like the ceo. assess the repo everytime . implement ways which can bring us(business) growth & money at same time helps users making win-win . instruct delegate the other open models what they should do .give proper instructions. they will just blindly execute whatever decision you have taken .meritocracy should win anyday. win-win solutions for both business-users like apple great,reliable , high quality products for users great revenue for business"): every session Claude Code assesses the repo (the board, the newest decisions, what is live, what users hit), picks the moves that are win-win (more value for seekers and referrers and more revenue: a great, reliable, high-quality product and great revenue), and delegates them as packets precise enough to execute exactly as written. Inside the owner's decisions the CEO decides and orders the queue; a change to an owner decision (prices, plan contents, legal text, live money, the login rule, referrer fairness, D-040/D-055/D-056) still goes to the owner with a 1-10 rating (D-023). Meritocracy (D-032): open models execute the packet as written, but a builder who finds the packet contradicts the code or a decision stops and reports it with evidence (`--report blocked`), and the best evidence wins. | Owner messages, Oct 20 2026. Incidents: a session resumed D-034 from memory after another model had shipped it; a usage-limit stop left GO-LIVE-1 work only on one machine; the picker told the agent holding the red-main fix lock to wait on itself and gave a restarted agent new work while its own claim sat idle. | Amends D-083 (the picker) and queue rules 2–4 (plan §3b); amends D-058: Claude Code no longer builds or takes over lanes; D-058's reliability list stays binding; extends D-074 (roles) and D-082. Affects `scripts/next-task.mjs`, `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`, `scripts/nextTaskText.mjs`, `scripts/nextTask.test.ts`, every agent entry doc, `docs/BRIEF.md`, `docs/DELEGATION.md`, `docs/plans/README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, plan §3b. | synced in the same change; in effect now · Oct 26 audit, changed by: amended by D-109, D-195, D-200 and D-203: Claude Code builds too, nothing waits for one model or session, and any capable session may write packets and order rows |

| D-109 | 2026-10-21 | **Open models execute; Claude Code decides.** Owner, verbatim: "some tasks are not being taken by open models. you only do decision making paart whats right for us like ceo . dont block taasks let open models execute". Found blocking them on Oct 21: (1) the picker read each pull request's CI with its own GitHub call, so an agent without a token hit GitHub's 60-reads-an-hour limit (403) after a run or two, and one unreadable pull request stopped the picker for everyone; (2) the first red or blocked priority held every task below it, in every lane; (3) packets (architect tasks) went only to Claude Code sessions. So: (a) the picker reads every pull request's CI in one call and uses `gh auth token` when present; a pull request whose CI cannot be read skips only that task; (b) a red or blocked priority holds its own lane and the tasks that depend on it, never unrelated lanes; red `main` still holds everything (D-082); (c) any agent may write a packet from the decisions, making no ruling they leave open on money, sign-in, identity, privacy or prices (those go to the CEO as `--report blocked`); (d) Claude Code decides (direction, the order in `docs/plans/assignments.json`, rulings, unblocks) and never holds work for itself; Tier 1 reviews and landings go to any session that did not build, fix or review the work, as the picker already allowed. | Owner instruction, Oct 21 2026; the picker evidence above. | Amends D-085 (the hold rule, packets reserved for Claude Code, the CEO as the only Tier 1 reviewer) and D-074's architect role; D-082 and D-083 unchanged. | D-134 | 2026-10-21 | **Many agents on one goal: foundation chain, cluster lanes, dark landing, wave releases (decided under D-109 by Claude Code as CEO; changeable).** Owner, verbatim: "unecessarily things are getting blocked. let me know any methods or ways multiple agents can work on a task breaking into subtasks ... choose efficinet bug free methods of pushing code". A read-only study (eight agents) found D-048 phase 3 serial by construction: every row shared the lane `design` and the picker allows one open pull request per lane. So, for any goal that spans many pages or modules: (1) one foundation chain first owns every shared file (for D-048: the route table, the app shell, the flag module `client/src/lib/goalSurfaces.ts`, primitives, the test harness); (2) the rest splits into cluster lanes by shared code, not by audience, so up to 13 agents build at once and no two lanes edit the same file; (3) every piece lands dark behind its own flag (D-097, D-111) in new files, with only a three-line switch in the existing file (for re-skins, replaced on Oct 22 by D-149: in place, with a parity check and a review before landing), never editing or deleting an existing test outside its owned paths (retirement rows delete kit tests with their code); (4) pieces go live in waves through one-line release pull requests, signed off by an independent reviewer and the CEO against the captures after QA on the preview (decided under D-123), with the owner sent the captures and price-showing waves held for the money gates; (5) while any dependency of a task is not done, the picker hands out no work on it at all (no build, review, fix, landing or packet; the board shows it waiting), a waiting task never holds its lane, and tasks that share a plan row write status in their packet's Status section (all done in this change); a landing slot, file ownership with a scope check and an epic roll-up follow (`docs/plans/PICKER-PARALLEL.md`, built by the open models); (6) landing is a fast-forward to the exact green commit (`git push origin <sha>:main`), never GitHub's merge button or squash, because "require branches to be up to date" is off on the ruleset and a merge commit from a pull request behind `main` was never tested; a refused push means merge the latest `main`, re-run the gate and CI, and fast-forward again. This amended D-082 (4)'s "merge the pull request" until the owner turned that setting on, which he did on Oct 22 ("Yes, turn on 'require branches to be up to date'"; ruleset 25433342, strict status checks): GitHub now lets only an up-to-date branch merge, so the merge-commit button is safe again; fast-forward stays the printed way, and squash or rebase-merge is never used, because they replace the tested commits. On Oct 21, #360, #362, #381, #410 and #415 were landed with the merge button and without the independent review their tier needs; `main` stayed green, so the CEO queued post-landing reviews (D-048-p3-live-review, D-132-audit) instead of reverting. Ratings: page or cluster tasks with file ownership 8/10; wave releases 7/10; audience lanes 6/10; contract-first API/UI split 6/10; many agents on one file 1/10. The D-048 rows are in `docs/plans/D-048-phase-3-split.md`; they follow D-120's screen order and contracts (`docs/plans/GOAL_UI_MIGRATION.md`, whose manual claim table now points to the picker), and the split's open questions are decided there under D-123. | Owner instruction, Oct 21 2026; the study's evidence: App.tsx holds all 87 routes on one line, #360 and #361 conflict in 8 hunks, #360 deletes a kit test, the picker landed built pull requests regardless of dependsOn. | Applies D-048, D-097, D-109, D-111, D-120 and D-123; the D-048 rollout map (#358) yields to it where they differ. The old money paths are switched off, never deleted (D-042), and anyone holding a pass, plan or credits keeps what they paid for. D-124's ladder is D-132's ladder; D-110 and D-117's levels, D-122's one price book, D-127's value moments and D-128's names stand; D-128's hold on plan tiers ends with D-132. (2) **Mentor.** D-132 priced Mentor (Rs199 / $2.99 a month, first 60 days free) and kept it after the owner's 19:15 reversal of the per-accepted-ask referrer credit (commit 5eee47c5, "Mentor unchanged"). Its fee coming back as earned credits when a mentor handles 2 asks is, in substance, a credit for handling asks, which frozen D-055 forbids, and a Mentor fee charges referrers, which needs the owner's word (D-119, D-123). So the CEO holds Mentor: its terms stay as data, nothing shows or sells it, and the owner rules on it as its own item. (3) **Per-level spend keeps the money rules.** An ask spends its level's credits once: at send, or at first visibility for an ask that waited for coverage (D-034). At send to a covered company, too few credits means no ask and nothing spent: the server answers with its quote and the screen offers the one-tap top-up; only a waiting ask that becomes visible is held when the seeker is short (D-047). A refund puts each credit back once, to the bucket it came from, and the reversal row records what came back. Today only pack credits always come back; plan cycle credits, free monthly credits and promo credits come back only while their cycle, month or grant is open. Under D-132 every paid credit, the in-plan rung credits included, never expires and so always comes back; free monthly and promo credits keep today's rule. With the flag on, a released or reopened ask keeps its stored level cost and Send again costs the job's level, not 1 credit: this amends D-035's and D-076 (1) and (6)'s one credit at enable. The daily money check (D-065) compares each ask's spend with its stored cost. (4) **Reach** (Fast Track up to 2 levels above the profile, Insider up to 4) sells access to more senior referrers, close to frozen D-006's never-sell "priority, visibility" and to D-040's "inbox placement" (a paying seeker's ask reaches inboxes an Open Door ask cannot). It is the owner's explicit choice (D-117, D-132), so it stands under D-126, flagged plainly: no referrer is ranked, shown first or paid, and no referrer sees a seeker's plan (D-006). (5) **What landed, and the check before enable.** #381 (job levels), #410 (plans page and plan family config) and #415 (per-level spend, pre-send quote, refund fix) reached `main` on Oct 21 with their flags off and without the independent review Tier 1 needs. Two parts of #415 run in production without a flag: its refund change (one reversal row per spend row, keyed `<id>#refund#<n>`, and a spend lookup widened from 6 to 32 rows) and the quote route. The CEO reviewed both on Oct 22: refunds stay idempotent (a spend already reversed under either key is found through `reversesTransactionId`), no code reads the old `<id>#refund` key, the change is what multi-credit asks need (one reversal per spend row when a spend draws from several buckets), and with the flag off every ask has one spend row, so live refunds work as before apart from the reversal key; the quote route needs sign-in, changes no balance beyond what `GET /api/credits/summary` already does (creating a missing seeker wallet and reconciling its cycle), answers 1 while `ASK_LEVEL_COST` is off and is sent with no-store. Nothing else of them is live. Before any of those flags is switched on, D-132-audit (Tier 1, `docs/plans/D-132-pricing-v2.md`) checks that code against (1)-(4), fixes what fails red-first and passes an independent review; it runs in lane `money-core` after D-035 (the same spend and refund code), D-048-p3-live-review (the explore cost line), TASK13-pinned-work (the same ask-creation transaction and migration numbering) and D-065-C (the money check it extends, which today cannot see delivery spends). PRICING-pack-ladder (#380) left the queue: D-132's rungs replace its 1/5/10/25/50. (6) **D-063's sentence** reads as the live code has shipped it since #396: "3 free credits every month; extra asks use credits. Referrers are never paid and never ask you for money." | D-149 | 2026-10-22 | **Goal-UI re-skins land in place, with a parity check and a review before landing (decided under D-123 by Claude Code as CEO; changeable; replaces D-134 (3) for re-skins).** (1) **What happened.** On Oct 21-22 seven surfaces moved to the goal UI by replacing their page in place, live, with no flag: `/requests` (#360), `/explore` (#362), `/queue` (#426, #428), `/explore/:slug` (#429), `/ask` (#431) and the seeker side of `/conversation` (#432, #433), besides the signed-out landing behind env flags (#396, #407, #409). Each was squash- or merge-button-landed with green CI and no independent review. None used the dark landing D-134 (3) planned; its foundation (`goalSurfaces.ts`, `RELEASED`) was never built. Main stayed green, but two live regressions shipped: a personal-link ask from a signed-out seeker reaches the whole company (#431, P0), and `/queue` records the referrer's official-process attestation without showing it (#426, P1); and the kit tests lost behaviour pins (the setup hand-off, slot counters, the attestation gate). (2) **Ruling.** A goal page may replace its kit page in place when it is a real goal redesign that meets the doctrine and its screen contract (D-150: a recoloured kit layout is not a move) and, on the pull request: (a) every function of the kit page (what a user can do and what the server receives) is kept or listed as consciously dropped with a reason; (b) every behaviour assertion in the kit tests it rewrites or deletes is ported, or listed with that reason; (c) every money line comes from `shared/moneyCopy.ts`, never retyped; (d) 1280 and 390 captures come from fictional fixtures; (e) a surface that shows or moves credits, sends an ask, signs in, shows private data or records a referrer's decision gets an independent review **before** landing (D-074), and lands by fast-forward or the merge-commit button, never squash (D-134 (6)). New behaviour, prices and money changes still ship behind a flag (D-097). For a page moved in place there is no switch, release row or kit retirement: rollback is a revert. (3) **Why.** The evidence is adoption: in-place moved seven surfaces in two days and the dark-landing foundation moved none, and D-111 asks for building to go live. The two regressions came from landing Tier 1 pages unreviewed and dropping kit tests, not from landing in place, so the ruling keeps the review and makes the parity explicit. Rating 7/10. The strongest argument against: in place removes the kit fallback, so a regression found after landing needs a revert instead of a one-line switch, and nothing is checked on a preview before users see it; both regressions above reached users that way. (4) **Queue.** P0-ask-link-signin and P1-queue-attestation (`docs/plans/LIVE-OCT22-regressions.md`) go to the top. D-048-p3-live-review landed on Oct 22 (#472) with its first-scope checks of `/explore` and `/requests`; three redesign-and-review rows then cover all seven surfaces: D-048-p3-live-review-find (`/explore`, `/explore/:slug`, `/ask`), D-048-p3-live-review-asks (`/requests`, the seeker `/conversation`) and D-048-p3-live-review-queue (`/queue`). Amending D-135 (5)'s order on Oct 23: D-132-audit no longer waits on D-035, which D-147 holds for the founder, because D-146 makes the audit next; the find and seeker-conversation rows wait for the audit instead, since it edits their cost lines first, and ROW20-CD's Hired link reaches the live conversation before its redesign. | The inventory of #360, #362, #426, #428, #429, #431, #432 and #433 against origin/main 635392fc (four agents, file:line); `client/src/pages/explore/useAskComposer.tsx` (`goToSeekerSignIn("/ask")`), `client/src/pages/MyCompanyInbox.tsx:160` (`ethicsConfirmed: true`), `server/privateReferralRoutes.ts:1232`. | D-151 | 2026-10-23 | **A Tier 1 review is independent only when its model neither built nor fixed the work (decided under D-074 by Claude Code as CEO; changeable).** (1) **The rule.** D-074 (3) asks for "an independent review by a different model session" and `docs/DELEGATION.md` §6 for "a different session from the implementer, ideally a different model". Read together: an agent's model is the part of its name before " · " (`modelOf` in `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`), and a session of a model that built, took over or pushed a fix to a Tier 1 pull request neither reviews it nor lands it after a passed review. Any other model's session may, including the reviewer who wrote the findings, who is best placed to check they were fixed. Two sessions of one model share its blind spots, so a same-model pass is not independent evidence. (2) **D-106 (#382).** Built by Instinct; its findings were fixed by Qoder twice (Qoder · d713 after workbuddy · 8dc3's review, Qoder · 31dd after DeepSeek V4.1 Flash Free · b072's review, head `7ee3f97e`). The picker then handed the re-review to Qoder · a236, because `did()` compares exact session names and #382 was built and fixed outside the picker's claim and fix markers, so the picker did not know who had worked on it. Qoder · a236 was right to post no verdict and report it blocked. The re-review goes to a model that has not pushed to `agent/instinct/share-impact-card` (not Instinct, not Qoder); the CEO takes it as its next Tier 1 review (D-085 lets the CEO review). The branch now conflicts with `main`: the merge is fix-stage work after the verdict, and a resolved conflict gets its own look before landing (the Oct 23 merge-policy amendment). (3) **Enforcement.** PICKER-review-independence (Tier 2, lane `picker`, `docs/plans/PICKER-PARALLEL.md`) makes the picker compare models, so a same-model session is never handed such a review or landing. Until it lands, a session handed one checks the board and the pull request for its own model and, finding it, posts no verdict and reports it blocked, as Qoder · a236 did. Rating 8/10: it costs speed when few models are active, because a model with many sessions cannot review its siblings' fixes; it buys what D-074 asked review for, a pass from eyes that did not write the code. The strongest argument against: sessions of one model are already independent runs, and a session-level rule unblocks faster; but the #382 chain shows the cost of the looser rule is a Tier 1 privacy surface passed by the model that wrote its fixes. | Qoder · a236's blocked report (Oct 22, 16:52 IST) and the D-106 report chain on `agent/reports/log`; the PR #382 review comments; `did()` and `modelOf` in `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`. | Clarifies D-074 (3) and `docs/DELEGATION.md` §6; affects `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`, `scripts/nextTaskText.mjs`, `scripts/nextTask.test.ts`, `docs/plans/PICKER-PARALLEL.md`, `docs/plans/OCT21-BUILT-PRS.md` and `docs/plans/assignments.json`. | adopted — sync; picker enforcement queued |

| D-182 | 2026-10-24 | **Cloudflare's Workers Builds check holds no landing and must not become a second production deploy path; #602 is released (decided under D-074 and D-085 by Claude Code as CEO; (3) is an ask for the owner; changeable).** (1) **What the check is.** `Workers Builds: skipwaitmanus` is Cloudflare's own Git integration, not GitHub Actions. On a branch it runs `wrangler versions upload` and stays green; on a `main` merge it runs `wrangler deploy`, which builds `./Dockerfile` and stops at `COPY commit-sha.txt`, because that file is gitignored and written only inside `deploy-api.yml` (diagnosis in #607). So it is red on `main` merges (`02a8a028`, `1c14dfef`) while the three required checks of the ruleset `main: green CI only (D-082)` (`typecheck`, `unit + component + route tests`, `production build`) and the real deploys stay green. It is neither required nor the deploy path. Ruling: a red Workers Builds check holds no landing, lane or report; a lander states it in one line and watches CI, Deploy API and Deploy Web to the end, as D-082 already asks. (2) **#607 does not land.** It makes that `wrangler deploy` succeed, which turns Cloudflare's builder into a second production writer of the API container: it would deploy on every `main` merge, docs-only ones included, without `migrate-db` (schema-dependent code could go live before its migration), without the release stamp (health would report the SHA `unknown`, so the exact-release check and the drift watch lose their anchor), and racing the serialized `deploy-api.yml` run (AGENTS.md: serialize production writers so latest `main` wins). Today the failing build is what keeps that path shut. (3) **Ask for the owner, 9/10:** in the Cloudflare dashboard, turn off production deploys from the Workers Builds Git integration of `skipwaitmanus` (disconnect the repository, or give it a deploy command that uploads a version without deploying it), so the red check goes away for the right reason. Against: disconnecting also drops Cloudflare's preview build on pull requests; nothing in the gate reads it. (4) **#602 (PICKER-board-newer-report) is released.** Its own "Landing hold" waited for that check, and GPT · 8d50 then read the hold as an instruction of its run; no decision holds it. It is Tier 2 with an independent Tier 2 pass and all four exact-head checks green on `2d2d1159`, so it lands the normal way: merge current `main`, required CI green on that head, then a fast-forward or the merge-commit button (D-134 (6)). Rating 9/10: it ends a hold that three lanes (#528, #565, #602) treated as real. The strongest argument against: a check left red trains everyone to ignore red; (3) removes it. | Check runs on `main` `02a8a028` and `1c14dfef` (Workers Builds failure, required checks success) against branch heads (success); `gh api` rulesets 25433342 and 25733248; `.github/workflows/deploy-api.yml` (`migrate-db` before the deploy, `commit-sha.txt` written in the workflow); `Dockerfile` line 18; the bodies of #607 and #602; GPT · 8d50's report of Oct 24, 04:00 IST. | Clarifies D-082 and the Oct 23 merge-policy amendment (required checks only); affects #602, #607, #528 and #565. | adopted — (3) waits for the owner |

Company search is a client filter over 39 hand-seeded companies. A request cannot carry a picked job. (2) **Ruling: sources, display and level.** Greenhouse first, then Ashby and Lever: documented, free, no key. Each company's board is used only after an admin records a link from its own careers domain; a 404, `[]` or zero result means unverified, never "0 jobs". Fetched jobs get their own table (never `jobs`, which holds private placeholder rows and feeds `JobPosting` markup), a snippet, the canonical apply link, a source-and-date line, no completeness claim unless the source list is complete, and no `JobPosting` markup. "whoever sign-up they will be able to see" is narrowed, as a CEO reading, to what costs nobody anything: a verified employee sees how many requests wait at their company (a count only), a pool delivery sends the referrer the review email a fresh request gets (at most one a day, D-060), and a lapse or pass tells the colleagues who can now see it; blocked pairs never get either. (4) **Ruling: no expiry never spends a forgotten request.** D-061 already rules that a request sent more than 30 days earlier is reconfirmed before its credit is spent at first visibility; it was never built, and D-174 removed only the closures. It is built now, in `deliverWaitingAskTx` (every delivery path), with no closure: an unconfirmed request keeps waiting and never closes, and confirming spends under the same key. Because removing the 90-day cap would otherwise let the hourly sweep charge a months-old held request the moment a credit lands, `D-174-expiry-clock-removal` waits for `D-174-held-ask-reconfirm` and goes live only with `ASK_RECONFIRM_ENABLED` on. In-app notice only until a D-060 email exists. (5) **Ruling: ownership.** The journey UI on `/explore`, `/explore/:slug` and `/ask` belongs to `D-174-seeker-journey-build` (`docs/plans/GOAL_UI_COVERAGE.md` §2 updated, D-167). New server work never rides a UI row. (6) **Defects found while mapping:** #612 loses a picked posted role across sign-in and can carry a stale one into a later request (latent: zero posted roles in production); production `/jobs` publishes a fabricated acme.com listing (D-001 lasting rule); the signed-in Home and the `/explore` description claim availability that production does not have, against D-176 and D-042. (8) **Asks for the owner (defaults hold until the owner answers):** charging at pickup so every verified employee can see waiting requests (8/10; against: it reopens D-047's no-free, no-debt rule); returning a free credit refunded after its month ends as a 30-day grant (6/10; against: unbounded roll-forward of free credits); SmartRecruiters despite its robots rule (5/10); crawl-class sources such as SuccessFactors sitemaps and Workday (4/10). | D-212 | 2026-10-26 | **Enterprise discipline at startup speed: every role is a picker stage or a machine check, a release counts as shipped only when all seven lights are green, and a red live release holds every landing but its fix (owner's instruction; the design decided under D-085, D-166 and D-200 by Claude Code as CEO; changeable).** (1) **The owner's words** (Oct 23): "our platfrom should have enterprise like organisation with startup speed ... have testers, have qa, have integrators, have reliability enginners, have devops, someone to see all lights are green or not fullstack then only something will be pushed ... if something is not working our systems should know and has to be worked fixed not untill user faces an error and drops off" and "there are so many branches, can you please consolidate once done ... just have only working branches, once done it should be merged"; on Oct 26 he chose to keep unmerged branches for now and delete only merged ones. (2) **Why.** D-166 closed the second door to `main` and makes the Tier 1 review a CI check; it does not cover testing, QA, release verification or production monitoring, and those are where the evidence points: #431 removed 107 assertions from `client/src/pages/ask.test.tsx` and nothing noticed; two live regressions (`LIVE-OCT22-regressions.md`) answered 200, so no error monitor could see them; the Oct 22 API outage was found and fixed by hand, and its 5-minute GitHub watch ran 24 times in 79 hours where about 950 runs were due; 32 of the 36 server files that answer a handled 5xx (`status(500|502|503)` in `server/**/*.ts` excluding tests; 4 of those 36 import `captureServerError` from `./sentry` directly — `jobCatalogRoutes`, `privateReferralRoutes`, `scheduledJobsRoutes`, `workosWebhooks` — and 11 non-test files import it in all, measured 2026-10-28 at `main` `0ae5dcde` and re-measured on `main` `622d1b0f`) never report to Sentry by that import, and the owner's 5xx email misses sign-in and webhooks; a review email to a referrer that fails is never sent again; both deploy workflows post their result on whichever issue is newest; `drift-watch.yml` keeps raising false drift (#687); `rollback.yml` has never run; and branches pile up because most landings are still fast-forward pushes that reach `main` through a ruleset bypass — read on 2026-10-29 without admin scope, `main-protection` (ruleset 25733248, `updated_at` 2026-10-27T09:37:55Z) lists `pull_request` and `required_status_checks` but returns no `bypass_actors`, while a direct `<sha>:main` push still succeeds, so a bypass of some scope exists and the `always` value first recorded on Oct 22 is unverified since the Oct 27 change. (3) **The model** (`docs/plans/ENTERPRISE-PIPELINE.md`). Tester: `GATE-test-integrity` (run from `scripts/scope-check.mjs`; a deleted or loosened test fails without a "Ported assertions" table, and pinned login and money tests cannot lose an assertion without a D-number). QA: `GATE-e2e-journeys` (six core journeys in a real browser against a MySQL service on every PR, which also applies the deploy migrations twice) and `PICKER-qa-stage` (the QA checklist runs inside the one Tier 1 review, and within 24 hours after landing for Tier 2 and 3 UI changes; never a second approval, D-200 (4)). Reviewer: `PROCESS-review-gate` (D-166) plus `GATE-tier-floor` (CI computes the tier from the paths). Integrator: the landing slot, file ownership and the queue gate (live); landing through the merge-commit button and the row's status set to done in the landing PR. Release manager: `REL-release-verify` (after both deploys it checks the web's newest deployed SHA and the `api-runtime` tag and runs a production smoke, sets `release/verified`, and opens one `incident` issue on failure) and `REL-rollback-skew` (the web waits for the API it needs; rollback works for web and API). Reliability engineer, knowing: `REL-watchdog` (a separate Cloudflare cron every 5 minutes, the external check D-100 approved, by email and, with the owner's token, a GitHub issue), `REL-5xx-capture`, `REL-heartbeats`, `REL-client-capture`, `REL-webhook-auth-alarm`, `REL-ops-invariants`; fixing: `REL-self-heal` (a dead API container is redeployed once, automatically) and `REL-review-email-retry`. DevOps: `PICKER-fullstack-lights`. (4) **The seven lights:** built right, checked by someone else (Tier 1), landed cleanly, deployed, verified live, watched (watchdog and jobs), no open incident. Lights 4-7 run by themselves after landing. A red one on the live release withholds every landing except its fix; builds, reviews and packets go on (D-109, D-200), and a code-only release a landing broke is reverted first (D-082). (5) **Branches.** D-205 stands and BRANCH-HYGIENE-1/2 automate it; BRANCH-HYGIENE-2 also switches the picker's printed landing step from `git push origin <sha>:main` (an admin bypass, which D-200 bans) to `gh pr merge <number> --merge`, and makes the picker read every open PR and find a landed row by its PR head. The first backlog pass ran Oct 26: 176 merged branches deleted (473 → 298 on origin, 67 locally); unmerged work stays, as the owner chose. (6) **The owner's word is needed for** settings, tokens and the ruleset (D-200 (6)): `main-protection`'s admin bypass, unverified since the Oct 27 ruleset change (read on 2026-10-29 without admin scope returns no `bypass_actors` while a direct `<sha>:main` push still succeeds), limited to pull requests only; the merge queue (D-166 (5)(b)); squash and rebase merges off; secret scanning, push protection and CodeQL (free on a public repo); adding each new check to ruleset 25433342 after 3 green days on `main`; the watchdog Worker's ZeptoMail secret and, optionally, a repository token for issues and the self-heal dispatch; a Sentry alert rule on new and regressed issues; and the hard stop on the Cloudflare credits before synthetic journeys run on the QA preview (#383, D-204). (7) **Dropped to keep startup speed:** no change board or second approver, no new hosted staging, no review before landing for Tier 2 or 3, no hand-written run logs where a commit status exists, no guard that cannot fail, no coverage targets. (8) **Registry hygiene in the same change** (D-200 (2)): six rows whose PRs merged on Oct 23-24 are set to done (PICKER-review-independence #530, PROCESS-queue-gate #603, POSTMERGE-oct22-review #619, D-166-founder-decisions-sync #599, D-048-fix-start-ask-handoff #586, D-048-p3-post #578), because the picker no longer saw them and was handing them out again; D-179-ats-validation moves to its own lane so lane `process` is free; PICKER-decision-sync and BRANCH-HYGIENE-2 were considered for `owns` and do not get them here. Both packets do list paths — PICKER-decision-sync's at `docs/plans/PICKER-decision-sync.md:28` (`scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`, `scripts/nextTaskText.mjs`, `scripts/next-task.mjs`, `scripts/nextTask.test.ts`, `docs/plans/assignments.json` for its own `syncedTo` stamp and status, plus the docs in its step 7) and BRANCH-HYGIENE-2's under "Owned paths" in `docs/plans/BRANCH-HYGIENE.md` (the first four of those, `nextTaskCore.mjs` for the `localSyncIssues` note only) — and either would overlap PICKER-heartbeat-slot-safety, PICKER-landed-marker-continuity and PICKER-landing-wait-fallthrough with no dependency in either direction. Registering them therefore needs its own ordering ruling or a narrower `owns`, not a dependency edge chosen to silence the validator; both rows stay in lane `picker`, where `laneBusy` already serialises them. Recorded as an open follow-up, not done, and nothing is owed today: PICKER-decision-sync landed (`86559ba7`) and is `done`, so its `owns` would schedule nothing, and BRANCH-HYGIENE-2 is held for the owner under D-232. Rating 9/10: every gate answers an incident we actually had, all of it is free, none of it waits on a person, and the review half rests on D-166's gate. Against: the e2e job adds a MySQL service and about 8 minutes to CI; review and QA markers are self-reported names under one GitHub identity (D-200: no second account), so auditable rather than forge-proof; and most of the board's power depends on owner settings in (6). | D-223 | 2026-10-27 | **Work moves as subtasks, not whole priorities: each priority is split into rows that one agent finishes alone, they land one by one, and a release row switches them on (decided under D-109 and D-203 by Claude Code as CEO; changeable).** Owner, verbatim (Oct 22): "plan for efficient execution. you(claude decide decisions) , divide priority wise taks,subtasks. let agents work on subtask over task, because holding a complete priority agents are asking and again that some agent is blocked by the task. so break tasks, let agents build subtasks. then merge". D-134 answered the same complaint on Oct 21 for D-048. This entry makes it the rule for every priority, and D-163 already applied it to the sign-in lane. (1) **The unit an agent claims is a subtask row.** It has an id `<parent>.<slug>`, its own branch and lane, `owns` listing the files it may touch, and its parent as `epic`. Its `dependsOn` names only the rows whose output it needs, never an ordering preference or a whole parent. `validateAssignments` refuses two open rows whose `owns` overlap unless one waits for the other, and `filesBusy` holds a build while an open row owns its files (PICKER-owns-scope). (2) **One agent session and one pull request each.** Each row lands on `main` by itself on green required CI (D-082, D-200), either behaviour-preserving or behind a code constant that starts false. A Tier 1 row gets its own small independent review (D-151). (3) **The merge is the parent's release row.** It depends on every piece and turns the switch on in a small pull request, after QA on the preview, a review and the CEO's sign-off. A switch that is owner-level (prices, legal text, live money, the login rule, referrer fairness) or hard to undo waits for the owner (D-123). (4) **A packet or an audit that already lists rows never gates a build.** The CEO, or any capable session (D-200), adds the rows directly, and an audit's findings come back as fix rows. (5) **A re-plan never takes an in-flight claim.** A row with a claimed branch or an open pull request keeps its id, branch and scope; further work becomes new rows. (6) **Split by owned files, never by step.** Keep the hot shared files (`client/src/App.tsx`, `client/src/pages/explore/useAskComposer.tsx`, `server/db.ts`, `server/privateReferralRoutes.ts`, `server/_core/index.ts`) in as few rows as possible, and prefer an existing mount point to a new registration line. (7) **Applied today: D-100.** Its unwritten packet row becomes ten rows (`docs/plans/D-100-security-audit.md`): four read-only audits, provider reconciliation and the OTP HMAC are parallel rows, and five of them can start now; the OTP HMAC waits for FIX-device-signin, which edits the same file; the CSP report, enforce, release and HSTS preload rows run in order after FND-4; the CSP report is answered inside `globalSecurityHeaders`, so no row edits `server/_core/index.ts`. Because the repository is public, an audit never publishes an unfixed exploitable finding: it goes to a private GitHub security advisory, or to the CEO with no details. HSTS preload, the production schema dump, backup settings and webhook confirmation stay with the owner. On `origin/main` `53155fde` this moves startable rows from 49 to 53. (8) **Rating 8/10.** It is above 7 because it turns waiting into parallel rows with no new picker code (`owns` and `epic` are on `main`), and every risky step keeps its review. The strongest argument against: more rows mean more pull requests, CI runs and landings, and a split that cuts one change across the hot files only adds hand-offs. That is why (6) splits by files, never by step. | Owner message, Oct 22; the Oct 23 board (15 of 29 open rows waiting, GPT · 5deb's six blocked reports on D-048-phase-3); `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs` (`owns`, `filesBusy`, `epic`); counts of startable rows on `origin/main` `53155fde` against this change. | Applies D-109, D-134, D-163, D-200 and D-203. | D-240 | 2026-10-30 | **`REL-release-verify`'s open question clears: a failed or partial deploy completion is smoke-checked against what actually went live, so the row's own planner `scripts/releaseSmokeCore.mjs` (with `scripts/releaseSmoke.test.ts`) stops skipping a trigger that did not succeed; this amends only D-238 (4) and (5) and packet items 2 and 3, and both runtime-only verify conditions, the #748 safety pins and the marker step stay exactly as they are (unblock ruling under D-200, D-032, D-212 and D-223 (1), in D-238's format, by a Hark session that neither raised the question nor holds the task and took no picker claim; changeable).** (1) **The question, reproduced.** Hark · 6699 holds draft PR #853 (branch `agent/queue/rel-release-verify`, head `16a84a7e`, required CI green) from landing on one question (its board report): D-238 (5) and packet item 3 say `release-verify.yml` runs on a failed deploy's completion and checks what actually went live, but at `16a84a7e` `planVerification` (`scripts/releaseSmokeCore.mjs:270-289`) returns `skip` at `:277` whenever `trigger.conclusion !== "success"`, and the branch's own test pins that skip ("a deploy that did not succeed is left to its Deploy failed issue", `scripts/releaseSmoke.test.ts:200-203`). Measured by importing that module at `16a84a7e` with a Deploy API trigger, a successful Deploy Web run and an `api-runtime` tag line: `failure`, `cancelled` and `timed_out` each return `{"action":"skip","reason":"Deploy API (Cloudflare Containers) ended <conclusion>; its Deploy failed issue reports it"}`, and only `success` returns `verify`. So D-238 (5)'s "failures included ... opens `Release not verified: <sha8>`" is not what the branch does: the `Deploy failed` incident says a run failed, never whether production still serves a healthy release after it. (2) **Ruling.** D-238 (4) and (5) and packet items 2 and 3 now read: `release-verify` plans a check for every `workflow_run` completion of either deploy on `main` whatever its conclusion (`success`, `failure`, `cancelled`, `timed_out`), and checks what is live against the existing expected SHAs: web = the head of the newest successful Deploy Web run, API = `refs/tags/api-runtime`, which a failed API run never moves. A completion that did not succeed never sets `release/verified` success on its own `head_sha` (that commit did not ship); a failing smoke or a SHA mismatch after it opens or updates the one `Release not verified: <sha8>` incident for the SHA the live release was expected to serve, and a passing smoke after it sets no success status on the failed head (its `Deploy failed` incident still speaks for it). **Partial deploy:** when one deploy kind for a commit succeeded and the other did not (API live at the new SHA but web not, or the reverse), each side is checked against its own expected SHA, so the side that moved is verified at the new SHA and the side that did not at its last good SHA, and neither side's failure is reported as the other's. **Supersession:** a newer run of the same kind, or any run of the other kind, still queued or in progress supersedes the completion (the existing `supersededBy`), which exits without a status or an issue, and two completions that check the same live SHAs update one issue, never open a second. (3) **Red-first tests (required, in `scripts/releaseSmoke.test.ts`, each failing on `16a84a7e` before the planner changes):** (a) failed deploy: an API trigger ending `failure`, `cancelled` or `timed_out` plans a live check with web = newest successful web head and API = the unmoved tag, and never marks the failed `head_sha` verified; (b) partial deploy in both directions: API `success` with web failed for the same commit checks the API at the new SHA and the web at the previous successful head, and web `success` with API failed checks the web at the new SHA and the API at the unmoved tag; (c) supersession: a newer queued or in-progress run of the same kind, or any of the other kind, supersedes a failed completion with no status and no issue, and two completions over the same live SHAs yield one issue title, so no duplicate or false incident. The skip test at `:200-203` is replaced by (a): the file is new on this branch (absent on `main`), so GATE-test-integrity has no landed assertion to protect there. (4) **Why it is narrow.** One decision branch of one pure function the row already owns, plus its tests; `scripts/release-smoke.mjs` and `.github/workflows/release-verify.yml` (both already owned) change only as far as carrying the planner's new outcome to the existing status and issue steps. No `owns` change, no new file, no workflow `if:` line, no new dependency; the row stays Tier 2. (5) **What it does not change.** Both `if: steps.changes.outputs.classification == 'runtime'` conditions on "Verify administrator sign-in gate" and "Verify active Cloudflare container release" (`deploy-api.yml:190,203` at `16a84a7e`); `id: deploy`; the "Update deployed runtime marker" step, so a failed deploy never moves `api-runtime`; the Notify `Deploy failed: <workflow>` rewrite; the #748 safety pins `scripts/requireGreenRelease.test.ts`, `scripts/require-green-release.mjs` and `scripts/assert-api-workflow-selection.sh`; `P1-release-gate-strict-compare`'s state; and D-238 (5)'s last sentence — the deploy job's own acceptance gate still does not run after its deploy step failed. (6) **Not an owner question.** No price, plan content, allowance, legal text, money path, login rule or referrer fairness moves, and nothing touches production: the smoke stays read-only. (7) **State.** `REL-release-verify` keeps its packet, `tier 2`, `status: "todo"`, `dependsOn: []` and `owns`, with `syncedTo: D-240`; Hark · 6699 keeps its claim and PR #853, and this ruling edits nothing on that branch. (8) **Validation.** `validateAssignments` reports no error on this change and `scripts/decision-id-check.mjs` passes; D-239 (#849, merged as `3ae94af2`) is the highest entry on `main`, so this entry takes D-240. | PR #853 head `16a84a7e` and Hark · 6699's board report; `scripts/releaseSmokeCore.mjs:270-289` (`:277`) and `scripts/releaseSmoke.test.ts:200-203` at `16a84a7e`; the module run named in (1); `.github/workflows/deploy-api.yml:177-178,189-190,199-203,217-218` at `16a84a7e`; D-238. | Amends D-238 (4) and (5) and `REL-release-verify` packet items 2 and 3 and its Tests list. Affects tasks: REL-release-verify. Touches no code, price, plan, legal text, login rule, money path or #748 pin. | adopted — docs-only ruling; the build resumes on #853 under the amended items |

| D-245 | 2026-10-30 | **The `D-057-session-gate` block clears with the owner's answer: a personal-login session reaches referrer actions only after a fresh work-email code taken in that same session. The code is proved by a dedicated server-side step-up record bound to the account, the session, the exact work email and the purpose. It never goes through the standalone code login, which creates another account (owner/CEO answer in chat, Oct 30, relayed by the Hark main session and recorded under D-200, D-032 and D-202; the owner's newer word under D-059's freeze of D-030/D-049/D-057, which it keeps; the design details in (4) are changeable).** (1) **The question.** `Hark · 450f` (report `1f344276`, task-branch status line `629cd79a`, no code written) found that the packet's §0 item 8 fails the main referrer setup path. A Google-first person P who verified a work email on `/verify` (`completeWorkEmailOtpEnrollment`, `server/db.ts:1537`: profile only, no alias) gets a new account Q from the code login (`server/_core/otpLogin.ts:65`). P's AuthKit Bearer still wins (`server/_core/workosAuth.ts:200`), so the gate sees identity P plus a code cookie of Q, refuses it, and loops for ever. The question offered options (a) to (c). (2) **The owner's answer, in substance.** Keep the adopted rule: a personal login may show existing records, but referrer actions need a fresh work-email code in that same session. The fresh verification binds securely to the authenticated account and the current session and keeps that account's history and referrals. An already-signed-in person is never sent through the standalone code login that creates another account; signed-out work-email sign-in stays separate and working. Forbidden: dropping the Google/WorkOS token, accepting a different account's cookie, matching people by company domain, inferring verification from a login identifier, and merging accounts. A dedicated server-side proof bound to account, session, exact work email and purpose is required; an old profile verification alone is not enough. The employer-change, logout, suspension and revocation rules stay. Red-first tests: the Google-first account completes the step-up without changing accounts; different-account, same-company and different-session attempts are refused; an expired or replayed proof is refused; logout and revocation invalidate it; seeker access is unchanged. The build goes through the normal independent review, and the full browser path is shown before anyone calls it fixed. Any remaining conflict is recorded on this row, never as a new overlapping task. (3) **Rejected:** (a) matching on the code cookie's `workemail_<email>` subject (it reads a login identifier and accepts another account's cookie); (b) dropping the AuthKit Bearer on referrer surfaces; (c) linking or merging P and Q first, or a switch until `D-057-referrer-continuity` lands. Continuity stays its own row and the gate does not wait for it. (4) **The packet amendment** (`docs/plans/D-057-session-gate.md` §0b, which wins over §0 to §8 where they differ): **Session key.** The session key is `workos:<sid>` from the verified Bearer or hosted cookie. Otherwise it is `app:<ssid>`, a new stable id in the app JWT (`server/_core/sdk.ts`) that renewal keeps. It is stored only as a hash. It is never taken from an openId, subject, email or anything the client sends. **Proof record.** A new table, `referrerStepUps`, with an additive migration at the next free number at commit time, recorded in plan §5. It holds the account id, the session-key hash, the purpose `referrer_actions`, the email fingerprint and domain, and its own code hash, so a sign-in code is never accepted and a step-up code never signs anyone in. The code lives 10 minutes with 5 tries; the challenge is single use; the proof lasts 12 hours and is never extended. **Freshness.** A proof is fresh only for that account and session key, while it is unexpired and unrevoked. It must also be issued after the account's effective `sessionsValidAfter`, while the account is not suspended, for the profile's current verified `workEmailDomain`, and not before `workEmailVerifiedAt`. So logout, revocation, suspension and employer change all invalidate it without a `server/db.ts` hook. **Exact address.** The address's domain must be the profile's verified domain. Once the account has an address of record (the fingerprint of its last proof since enrolment), the address must equal it. This is a check inside one account and never matches another account. **Endpoints.** Two new endpoints, `POST /api/referrer/step-up/send` and `POST /api/referrer/step-up/verify`, in a new `server/referrerStepUpRoutes.ts`. Neither calls `resolveLoginIdentity`, `saveVerifiedWorkEmail` or `completeWorkEmailOtpEnrollment`, or any link or merge function. Neither writes users, aliases, people or profiles, and neither mints `amr` or another account's session. **Gate.** `requireWorkEmailSession` (a new `server/referrerSessionGate.ts`) passes a code session (§0 items 1 to 8, unchanged) or a fresh proof. `/access` gains `codeRequired`, and the hook gains `code_required`. **Client.** A signed-in person sees an in-session step-up (`GoalWorkEmailStepUp`) on the eight referrer surfaces named in §0b.6, and a 403 `work_email_code_required` flips the page to it. `referrerSignInHref` is for signed-out people only. `UnifiedInbox.tsx` stays hidden on its referring side (fails closed) because `D-048-p3-inbox` owns it. **Tests and done.** The §0b.7 tests are listed one by one. The §0b.9 definition of done adds independent review and the full browser path at 1280 and 390. (5) **Conflicts recorded on the row, no new task.** The build needs four paths that other live rows own: `server/privateReferralRoutes.ts` (`D-090-BYOR`, draft PR #632, plus its chain `P0-referral-access-availability`, `D-132-item3`, `D-174-held-ask-reconfirm`, `D-174-ask-from-job` and `D-174-pool-nudges`), `server/_core/index.ts` (`D-090-BYOR`; switch-only for `D-132-item3` and `D-099-bonus-pages`), and `drizzle/schema.ts` and `server/schemaReconcile.ts` (`D-090-BYOR`, `D-099-bonus-pages`). Adding them to this row's `owns` fails `validateAssignments` unless one side waits for the other, which changes other live rows' schedules. That order stays open for the owner or CEO. Until it is ruled, the row keeps no `owns` (as before) and the reviewer checks the diff against §0b.8 by hand. (6) **State.** `D-057-session-gate` keeps tier 1, `status: "todo"`, `dependsOn: []`, its branch and `Hark · 450f`'s claim, with `syncedTo: D-245`. This ruling edits nothing on `agent/queue/d057-session-gate`. When that branch next merges `main`, the §3b row on `main` replaces its blocked line. The board's blocked report (`1f344276`) is cleared by the next report from a session that neither raised it nor holds the task, or by the owner; this docs PR took no picker claim and posted no report. (7) **Validation.** `validateAssignments` reports no error, and `scripts/decision-id-check.mjs` passes. D-244 is the highest entry on `main`, and D-242 is held by #858, so this entry takes D-245. | Owner/CEO answer in chat, Oct 30 2026 (relayed); block report `1f344276` and `agent/queue/d057-session-gate` `629cd79a` (merged with main as `d83a72a5`); code read on `main` `2892629a`: `server/_core/sdk.ts` (no stable session id; `jti` changes on renewal), `server/_core/otpLogin.ts:65,72`, `server/_core/workosAuth.ts:80,200,345`, `server/db.ts:267-277,1100,1265,1537,1588`, `server/workEmailOtp.ts`, `server/workEmailKey.ts`, `server/privateReferralRoutes.ts:652-720,926`, `server/profileRoutes.ts:132`, `client/src/pages/UnifiedInbox.tsx:30`; `owns` read from `docs/plans/assignments.json` on `main`. | Amends D-163's §0 item 8 ruling (its client half, and the case of another account's code cookie) and packet `D-057-session-gate.md` §1, §4.3, §4.5, §5, §6.5, §7 and §8. It delivers the session-gate half of the `D-057-referrer-continuity` row's "referrer actions in a personal-login session ask for a work-email code first", and leaves that row's linking, employer move and gap state unchanged. (3) **The ruling, the RECOVERY_SYSTEM question the block left open.** `scripts/require-green-release.mjs` is **not** widened and stays byte-identical. The API leg keeps `--target api --recovery`; the web leg runs the row's own `scripts/rollbackTarget.mjs web-gate` (already built in item 1, accepting `web` or `all` via `WEB_SYSTEMS`, reusing `ciVerdict`/`releaseRuns` from the trusted module **by import, never a copy**); a `target: all` dispatch therefore reaches *each leg's* gate as that leg's own system. `TARGETS` and `contextProblem` keep admitting `api` and `web`, so `--target all` stays an unknown target everywhere outside a recovery, and the non-recovery gates of `deploy-api.yml`, `deploy-pages.yml` and `release-verify.yml` see no new value and no changed verdict. The API recovery's schema-contract refusal stays as it is; a web recovery carries no schema contract and `web-gate` never claims one. The alternative seen on #882 — teaching the shipped gate a `web` system with its drift check limited to the API — is rejected here: it edits the module every production release runs through, to duplicate a contract `web-gate` already implements and tests (22/22), which is more change than this row needs and a larger blast radius than a Tier 2 row landing on green CI without an independent review should carry. (4) **The pins are extended, never loosened.** All three stay exact-set assertions — every option, every job key, every `GITHUB_TOKEN` step enumerated in order — so a fourth option or job that skips a pin update still fails; the negative lookahead becomes the positive three-value set (`api`, `all`, `web`) in the declared order. No assertion is deleted, no test is skipped, no `--no-verify`. `scripts/requireGreenRelease.test.ts` is not one of `PINNED_TESTS` (`scripts/testIntegrityCore.mjs:29-33`), so rule 5 does not apply, while rules 1-4 do. The workflow-level `concurrency: group: deploy-api` pin at `:546` stays true: the packet puts the web leg under a **job-level** `concurrency: { group: deploy-pages }`, so the API and web queues are never crossed. (5) **The phantom reservation, and why this is a release rather than a wait.** `P1-release-gate-strict-compare` also owned `scripts/requireGreenRelease.test.ts` (and `require-green-release.mjs`), and `filesBusy` (`scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs:552`) holds any build whose `owns` overlaps a row with `status: "in-review"`, so widening this row alone would not clear the block — a parallel ruling on branch `docs/d249-rel-rollback-skew-unblock` (PR #882, closed unmerged) reproduced exactly that. Its diagnosis is adopted because its evidence is better than a dependency edge: that row's work is on `main` (`fecf0d3b` is an ancestor of `origin/main`; its own report records #755 merged with its required CI green) and what remains is **item 5, an observation**, so a buildable row must not wait for a row that may sit `in-review` indefinitely, which is what a `dependsOn` edge here would have done. The landed row therefore **drops `owns`** and keeps `in-review`, re-declaring `owns` in a docs-only pull request if item 5 ever needs code (D-200 (2), the shape D-249 proposed). This row gains no `dependsOn` edge and takes only the pins' file: `scripts/require-green-release.mjs` stays unreserved because this ruling does not change it (3). (6) **Tier (GATE-tier-floor, D-212).** `.github/workflows/**` is Tier 2 in `scripts/tierClassifyCore.mjs` (`TIER2_PREFIXES`), any other non-docs file is Tier 2 (`classifyTier`), and a test file never raises the tier (`tierReasonPaths` skips `TEST_FILE`), so the row stays Tier 2 and lands on green required CI (D-200 (5)) with its own red-first tests. No owner decision — price, plan content, legal text, live money, the login rule, referrer fairness — is touched. (7) **Rejected.** (a) A separate `.github/workflows/rollback-web.yml` that would leave the pins untouched: it splits one deliberate four-input dispatch into two, breaks `all` as a single request and leaves REL-self-heal's `target: api` as the only dispatched recovery. (b) Adding `all` to the release `TARGETS`/`contextProblem` so one gate run covers both legs: that moves the release path every deploy depends on and makes `--target all` legal outside a recovery. (c) Deleting or loosening the three pins to get green: forbidden by D-082 and `GATE-test-integrity`. (d) Taking over the branch: item 1 stands, its builder's work is not rewritten, and the widened build continues on `agent/queue/rel-rollback-skew` with no second writer (D-200 (2)). (8) **What this is not.** Nothing in this ruling changes code, tests, workflows, prices, plan content, legal text, the login rule, a money path or a GitHub setting; #876 stays a draft and the row keeps `status: "todo"` so the build hand-out resumes. Every further takeover removes one more model from those that may review. (4) **Not nothing to fix: two candidate defects.** Instinct's bounded handoff on #922 (comment 6554152648, 05:56Z, after the block) asks the existing owner to qualify two residual cases. Traced in source at `54fafe74` (source reading only, no test run: UNVERIFIED until reproduced red-first): (a) *An empty read-only link.* The company-door return URL is `/explore/<slug>?open=1&post=<id>` with no `job` (`client/src/pages/explore/signInHandoff.ts:28-35`); `useAskComposer` seeds `postSelection` from `parseOpportunityAskSearch` (`client/src/pages/explore/useAskComposer.tsx:333`, `shared/companyOpportunities.ts:99`) as `{ opportunityId, jobUrl: null }`, so the link comes only from the sessionStorage prefill; the door opens the drawer on any `open` or `post` (`client/src/pages/ExploreCompany.tsx:82`); and the drawer renders the field `readOnly` whenever a post is selected (`client/src/pages/explore/AskDrawer.tsx:159`). Whenever the prefill is missing (storage lost, another tab, or any hand-made `?post=<digits>` link, not only a sign-in return) the seeker sees an empty read-only field and can continue only through "Change role" (`AskDrawer.tsx:173`), which drops the post; §5.1 asks for "the post's link read-only", which presumes a link. (b) *A stale selection after Close.* Close clears the in-memory selection and the `open`/`post` query (`ExploreCompany.tsx:89-93`) but not `skipwait-ask-prefill`, which is cleared only after a successful send (`useAskComposer.tsx:561`) and lives one hour (`client/src/lib/askPrefill.ts:13`). On `/ask` the prefill is applied whatever its company (`useAskComposer.tsx:365`) and now restores `opportunityId` (`:368`); `/ask` (`client/src/pages/Ask.tsx`, unchanged by #922) shows the first-look line (`Ask.tsx:161`) but has no "Change role", so a later `/ask` send in that tab within the hour carries the old id (`useAskComposer.tsx:504`). The server refuses a mismatched, inactive or expired opening inside its transaction (`server/db.ts:1906-1908`, "This opening is no longer available…"), so no money moves, but the seeker can be stuck on that error for up to an hour, and a same-company send would give the old post's owner the first look. The row's title says "never carry a stale one into a later request". These are candidates, not a verdict: the builder lineage fixes each red-first inside the row's `owns` or qualifies it against §5.1 on #922, and the independent reviewer judges both once review can be handed. So a snapshot or summary throw leaves `skipwait_head` behind, and a throwing drop puts its own error on stderr in place of the FAILED verdict (comments 6577133271 and 6578601856). That run uses fakes, not MySQL, and is preparation evidence, not a verdict or PASS. (3) **Contract and scope.** The contract of record is the coordinator-qualified W5 contract, "PR #967 cleanup contract: derived desired behaviour for the red-first candidate". The coordinator verified its original 11-file manifest and the W8 28-file manifest. Its desired behaviour is a derived test interpretation, not an owner ruling. The PR #967 comments it rests on are coordinator comments: GitHub's OWNER association on them is not evidence of human approval or an owner grant. The settled items are the packet's §4a-4b. On the failure paths CL-1 to CL-3c the requirement is at least one cleanup attempt. On the normal paths CL-5 it is exactly one. That is preserved, because it keeps the measured drop; any relaxation needs an actual owner ruling, and none is assumed. ER-3a/b require that the raised error keeps the FAILED verdict when cleanup fails. ER-7 settles only that the FAILED line is visible and the exit is non-zero. The following stay unsettled and are not decided by this row: U1 to U11, including the pass/warn and S1/S2/summary exit statuses, which error is raised in U3, U4 and U9/R15, the cleanup-error channel, the retry count, the summary contents (U8) and the ordering beyond ER-4 (U10). Reference-creation failure (U7) and the migration-base file (U11) are outside the scope. (4) **Corrections to the unregistered draft.** That draft called the contract unavailable; it is the W5 contract above. It said an 11b2 cleanup ACK existed; none is known, and comment 6581117289 is `Qoder · 11b2`'s landed-row handover, not a cleanup ACK. 11b2's availability and any unpublished overlap stay unconfirmed, so the builder probes for overlap at claim time. If the run id has to travel from `ciStateFromRuns`, carrying it there is inside `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`, already a packet file. No other hunk in `scripts/next-task.mjs`.
