| D-292 | 2026-11-07 | **The named CI step "Review gate (independent Tier 1 pass)" fails open on every pull request because nothing has fetched `origin/main` when it runs, so D-286/D-291's classifier can never fire; `REVIEW-GATE-fetch-parity` is registered (Tier 2, lane `process`) to give `scripts/review-gate.mjs` the queue gate's own `origin/main` fetch, keep its review-lock fetch, and leave `ci.yml`, `scope-check.mjs` and every required check unchanged (packet ruling, decided under D-200; cites D-286, D-291 and D-032; changeable).** (1) **Measured, not inherited.** PR #878 head `e1128076`, run `42339003779`, `typecheck` job `128302286336`: step 11 "Review gate (independent Tier 1 pass)" is `success` after printing "review-gate: could not run (Command failed: git show origin/main:docs/plans/assignments.json / fatal: invalid object name 'origin/main'.) — not failing the branch"; step 12 "Scope check (declared file ownership)" is `failure`, after `scripts/queue-gate.mjs:15-28` `ensureOriginMainHistory()` fetched `main -> origin/main` and scope-check's internal call (`scripts/scope-check.mjs:96-97`) printed "Tier 1: this head has no independent passing review … Nobody has reviewed D-089-request-inbox yet." Cause: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:41` checks out at depth 1 and `:49-69` fetches only `BASE_SHA` and `HEAD_SHA`; `scripts/review-gate.mjs:250` reads `origin/main` first and `:287-290` turns the throw into exit 0. `gateOnlyRedFromJobs` (`scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs:306-336`) is keyed on the named step, so on a real run it is always false and the picker still hands MAKE GREEN. Reproduced locally on a depth-1 fetch of `e1128076`: exit 0 with "could not run"; after the queue gate's fetch, exit 1 with the review verdict. (2) **Ruling.** `scripts/review-gate.mjs` gets a local copy of the queue gate's `ensureOriginMainHistory()` (precedent: `scripts/test-integrity.mjs:24`), called before its first `origin/main` read. Its existing review-lock fetch (`:234-239`, `:269-270`) stays and runs after it, so a post-review run does not false-red. Definite verdicts fail closed as today. Genuine fetch or read errors keep failing open, loudly: a closed failure there would show as a red at the named step and be misread by the classifier as "review missing", and scope-check's unchanged internal call re-enforces the same rule straight after. (3) **No double red, so no classifier or scope-check change.** The Scope check step's `if:` (`ci.yml:116`) carries no status function, so GitHub prepends `success()` and skips it whenever the named step failed; `gateOnlyRedFromJobs` accepts `skipped`. scope-check's duplicate call stays as the backstop for the named step's fail-open path, for a future `ci.yml` edit and for local runs. The accepted trade-off is that a hidden scope-check red shows only after the review passes, which is no worse than before #1008. The recorded alternative (reorder `ci.yml` and drop the internal call) is not taken; the owner or CEO may choose it (D-032). (4) **Files.** `scripts/review-gate.mjs` and a new `scripts/reviewGateFetchParity.test.ts` (red-first, real git, no network), which are the row's `owns`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `scripts/scope-check.mjs` and `scripts/queue-gate.mjs` are not touched. There is no new, renamed or removed required status check and no branch-protection or ruleset change. (5) **Acceptance.** The tests in packet §7, plus live proof on a real gate-only-red Tier 1 pull request (#878, or another Tier 1 head red only for its review): the only failed step is "Review gate (independent Tier 1 pass)", Scope check is `skipped`, and the picker prints REVIEW. (6) **What this is not.** No gate waived, downgraded, bypassed or skipped; no verdict, review, `Fixed` or landing marker; no status `done`; no reorder of existing rows (appended last); no product, runtime, price, plan-content, legal-text, live-money, login-rule, privacy or referrer-fairness change, so nothing here is owner-reserved. Rating 8/10: it is the smallest change that makes the D-286 cure observable on real runs. The strongest argument against is that hidden scope-check reds can cost one extra review cycle, which the recorded alternative avoids at the price of touching `ci.yml`. | Run `42339003779`, job `128302286336` (PR #878, head `e1128076`) step list and log, Sat 2026-11-07 10:04 AM IST (UTC+05:30); `.github/workflows/ci.yml:41`, `:49-69`, `:107-117`; `scripts/review-gate.mjs:234-239`, `:250`, `:269-274`, `:287-290`; `scripts/scope-check.mjs:76-79`, `:93-97`; `scripts/queue-gate.mjs:15-28`, `:98`; `scripts/test-integrity.mjs:24-34`; `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs:306-336`; local depth-1 reproduction against `main` `8e1be413`; D-286, D-291, D-032, D-200; D-292 checked free on `main` and on every `origin` branch (D-287 only on `docs/d287-admin-approvals-unblock`, D-289 only on `agent/architect/d089-request-inbox`). | Supersedes nothing. The picker's batch read (`scripts/next-task.mjs:285-293`, newest 100 `ci.yml` runs filtered by `head_branch` only) passed through the real `ciStateFromRuns` yields `{"state":"green","sha":"58e065cb3451ec6c50ea72c730ed1fc9b88836bd",…,"runId":42094576051}`; run `42094576051` is `"conclusion": "success"` on `"head_sha": "58e065cb…"`. The four commits after `58e065cb` are empty picker markers with no CI run, two of them `Takeover` markers written by LAND hands (comment `6697418224`: "The picker handed `LAND D-048-p3-admin-approvals` at 22:05Z and wrote two empty markers"). Current head `7aeaf8fb` has no CI workflow run (only Workers Builds)." (2) **Cause.** The exact-tip comparison exists only on D-291's gate-only path (`scripts/next-task.mjs:301-302`; `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs:607`). `ciStateFromRuns` (`scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs:288-299`) returns the newest completed run's state with its sha and nothing compares that sha for a green state; the view keeps only `ci.state` (`:584`, `:600`); the Tier 2 hand (`:783-786`) reaches LAND for every state that is not error, red or running, including `green` on any sha and `unknown`. The Tier 1 (`:744-747`), packet (`:797-801`) and `continue` (`:906`, `:915`, `:918`) LAND paths and the board's `pr-green` read the same value. (3) **Ruling.** One pure helper in `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`, applied where the view reads `ci`, makes a state landable only when it is `green` and its `sha` equals `info.tipSha` (the `:607` comparison); `green` on another sha, `unknown` with an open pull request, or an unreadable tip becomes `"no-tip-ci"`, and no path lands on it. Fail-closed hand: a Tier 2 row quiet for 2 hours gets the existing MAKE GREEN (`kind: "green"`) with a `why` saying CI has not run on the tip and asking for a two-parent sync push (never a rebase), because a bare skip would park a tip that marker pushes never gave a run, and MAKE GREEN already means green required CI on the exact head with its idle, avoid and claim rules; a row not quiet waits. Tier 1 waits as if CI were running; a packet pull request is not offered LAND; own-work `continue` becomes `build`; the board falls to `pr-open`; red is unchanged. `scripts/next-task.mjs` gets one hunk: when a head's batch state is not on its tip, one `GET /actions/workflows/ci.yml/runs?head_sha=<tip>` read replaces it, failing closed to no LAND on any error or empty answer. (4) **Files.** `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`, `scripts/next-task.mjs` (that hunk only) and a new `scripts/nextTaskLandExactHead.test.ts`; no `owns`, measured (`scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs` is owned by three unstarted picker rows, so the build serializes on the `picker` lane as D-286 (4) did). No `scripts/nextTaskText.mjs`, `ci.yml`, review-gate, scope-check or queue-gate change; no new, renamed or removed required status check and no branch-protection or ruleset change. (5) **Acceptance.** Red-first tests in packet §8 (green on a stale sha → no LAND; green on the tip → LAND; no run on the tip → no LAND; tip-read failure → no LAND; plus the Tier 1, packet, board, continue and `head_sha`-read wiring cases), and live proof that #994's row is not offered LAND until CI runs on its tip. (6) **What this is not.** No gate waived, downgraded, bypassed or skipped; no verdict, review, `Fixed` or landing marker; no status `done`; no change to #994's own state or holds; no reorder (appended last); no product, runtime, price, plan-content, legal-text, live-money, login-rule, privacy or referrer-fairness change, so nothing here is owner-reserved. The review-gate fetch-parity defect is D-292's (PR #1013) and out of scope. (3) **Precondition 3 answered from measured history (Oct 31 restriction).** The clause reads "only skipwait.me, no production changes, migrations, drills, settings changes or spending" (`docs/BUILD_CONTINUITY.md:52`). An ordinary protected-PR landing through exact required CI, with its automatic deploy, is therefore in bounds; the restriction's demonstrated bite is migrations (D-268 (3)), drills, settings and spending, none of which this row ships. This answers only this row and releases no migration, flag, env key, settings change, drill, spend, new row or general pause. (4) **Ruling: exact steps, in order.** On `agent/queue/d100-otp-hmac` (not on this docs branch): merge current `origin/main`; change only `OTP_HMAC_SINCE` to `"2026-12-01T00:00:00.000Z"`; every other reviewed line in `server/` and the tests stays byte-identical to the `fbec7ab5` review; update the packet Status line; push. Then a fresh independent Tier 1 review by a model other than builder `Hark · b473` and any fixer names the new head (`66b7a0dc` does not carry over a non-merge commit; D-151, D-074). Required CI green on that exact head; a LAND hand-out merge-commits it (D-134 (6)), never `692e7c3c`. After deploy: `verify-live-release.sh` on the landed SHA plus one referrer and one seeker email-code sign-in (precondition 4). (5) **The fixture admission, and its limits.** §6 admits `scripts/nextTask.test.ts`, `scripts/nextTaskArchitectLanding.test.ts`, `scripts/nextTaskAvoidLand.test.ts`, `scripts/nextTaskHeartbeatSlot.test.ts` and `scripts/nextTaskLandingWaitFallthrough.test.ts` for **fixture data only**: a `sha` on a green `prCi` entry equal to that branch's `tipSha`; a `tipSha` on a branch fixture that has none; an explicit `prCi` where a Tier 1 landing fixture omitted one; and, in the heartbeat harness's injected fetch, a completed successful `ci.yml` run whose `head_sha` is the disposable origin's real branch tip instead of `workflow_runs: []`. Forbidden in those five files: deleting, renaming, skipping, todo-ing or focusing a test; removing or loosening an assertion; changing any expected value, title or `PINNED_TESTS` entry; and any edit that is not the CI/tip data the fixture always meant. An omitted `prCi` must keep meaning `unknown`, so no wrapper may default an open pull request to green. `scripts/nextTaskGateRedReview.test.ts` and `scripts/nextTaskLandedMarkerContinuity.test.ts` pass under strict R1 as written (measured) and are **not** admitted. GATE-test-integrity still runs on the pull request and must pass. If a case cannot be made green without changing an expected value, stop and `--report blocked` naming that case: it would mean R1 changes behaviour the suite deliberately pins, and the owner decides (D-032). (6) **Bounds.** No `owns` field is added, measured: `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs` is owned by the unstarted `PICKER-fullstack-lights`, `PICKER-qa-stage` and `PICKER-landing-wait-fallthrough`, `scripts/nextTask.test.ts` by the first two, and `scripts/nextTaskLandingWaitFallthrough.test.ts` by the third, so an `owns` here would fail `validateAssignments` or park the fix behind unstarted work; the build keeps serializing on the `picker` lane (D-286 (4)). No `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, required-check, ruleset, branch-protection, `scripts/nextTaskText.mjs`, `scripts/review-gate.mjs`, `scripts/scope-check.mjs` or `scripts/queue-gate.mjs` change, and nothing under `server/`, `client/`, `shared/` or `drizzle/`. D-293 R2-R5, `ciStateFromRuns`'s contract, `mainCi`, the landing slot, D-151 independence and D-190 are unchanged. (7) **Validation on this ruling's head.** `validateAssignments` returns `[]`; `node scripts/decision-id-check.mjs` exits 0; this branch edits only `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `docs/plans/PICKER-land-exact-head.md` and `docs/plans/assignments.json`, so no runtime, gate, tier or `owns` change rides it. (8) **What this is not.** No code is edited by this ruling; no test is weakened, waived, skipped or retired; no gate, required check, protection or tier changes; no verdict or review marker is written; neither #1028 nor `ce23492f` is landed here; no row is created, re-tiered or marked `done`; #994 keeps every precondition in coordinator comment `6702703550`; and nothing touches prices, plan contents, legal text, live money, the login rule, privacy, referrer fairness, production, migrations or spending. Against: §6's "existing suites stay green as written" was this row's own guard against a fix that quietly redefines the picker, and fixture data is exactly where such a redefinition hides — the answer is that the admission is data-only, machine-checked by test-integrity and by the ban on changing any expected value, and the prototype shows all 287 picker assertions passing with their expectations untouched; the alternative, keeping the carve-out, ships a production hole to make fixtures convenient. ## D-284 correction: technical registration is not merge authority

PR #980 recorded technical ownership and described D-200 (4) as removing the need for a separate reviewer. The technical registration remains; its claim to waive the coordinator's actual permission conditions is withdrawn. Do not infer permission from the GitHub account, a commit author label, this registry status or green CI alone.

Provenance checked: D-200 originated in commit `734d6df1077aeda05b2746eaeaf5633190886d41`, PR #640, with the session reference `session_01WPKmDqN1thuMnyCePMuoDe`. Its archived human turns express speed, a public repo and no second GitHub account, and include: "instinct and cue by manus are helping me build . session timeout of you are blocking me . let these guys handle when your session is time off". That later message concerns work continuity while the Claude session is off; it states no merge or review condition. The specific Tier 2/3 no-separate-review clause appears as the session's interpretation, not an explicit human instruction recovered in that archive. This is a limit on the recovered evidence, not a finding of fabrication or proof that no other instruction exists. The quoted speed intent is not withdrawn. This correction does not settle a new global contributor merge policy or reopen D-200's other rules.

Instinct's authenticated routine-merge arrangement is narrower: a separate review passes on the final version, all required tests pass, and the work is checked against current main, with no admin bypass. Sources: offer `phonemsg-01M5PPN30MY236JNCE4EGMWHF1` (October 24, 2026, 15:47 IST), accepted by `phonemsg-01M5PPZKZ7NFECSZSVNXD8HDV3` (15:53 IST). No recovered instruction removes its separate final-version review condition. Keep those conditions for work coordinated under it, including #978 and this correction. Use the existing distinct normal-procedure lander. D-151's additional different-model restriction remains Tier 1-specific; no new blanket model restriction is created.

This correction changes no assignments, `syncedTo`, row status, tier, dependencies, runtime code, route map, AUDITED entry or release flag. The three registered paths remain available for bounded technical work after live ownership and unpublished work are checked. The reproduction counts and all-open-branch scan above remain the original author's reported evidence, not this correction's independently reproduced results. On the existing admin branch, any two behavior-only AUDITED entries and bounded `/admin-review` route correction must pass the applicable final-head review, tests and current-main checks before coordinated landing. No `/admin/activity` or other route expansion, history rewrite, automatic revert or new queue row is implied.

It also bears on `D-048-p3-profile` (D-258, Oct 31), which is parked for that reason and one more: its review lock is held by a model that took over the work branch 29 seconds before claiming the review (`3f582ee6` 15:05:13Z, `2dd5566a` 15:05:42Z), so the gate refuses the holder's pass as a builder (`scripts/review-gate.mjs:136,139`) and `scripts/next-task.mjs:325` refuses anyone else's verdict, while an eligible reviewer (`Qoder`) does exist. The technical qualification therefore also covers one extra clause: it must refuse a REVIEW hand-out to a claimant already in the row's `builders`/`fixers` set, and must not treat a review lock held by a builder as a lock. `PRICING-banded-oct25` (D-266, Nov 1) adds one fact worth your attention: its deliverable had been silently deleted from the branch by `993c7e78`, a commit labelled a merge of `main` that has only one parent, so for a day its green CI was the green of a pull request that no longer changed anything a reviewer could pass; it was restored on Nov 1 and every product check is green on `5116c114`.** **`FIX-device-signin` is the seventh member, and the cleanest measurement yet (D-273, Nov 1):** its closure pull request #952 is red on `typecheck` alone, failing inside the review gate while `unit + component + route tests`, `production build` and `e2e journeys` are green on the same head, so nothing but the gate stands between a finished, live row and `done`. It also shows the loop closing in the picker's own words: the hand-out said "CI on #952 turned red after its review passed; fix it red-first, then it is reviewed again", and `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs:573-584` can only ever answer `fix` or `green` while CI is red, so the promised second review is unreachable by construction. Six rows wait on this one and the code they wait for has been live since 2026-10-26 (`059ef908`), so the qualification's outcome matters to the launch chain behind it. **`D-086.count` is the eighth member (D-279, Nov 2):** its pull request #955 is red on `typecheck` alone, failing inside the review gate at step 11 while the job's own `Typecheck`, `Design tokens` and `Deploy trigger coverage` steps and the `unit + component + route tests`, `production build` and `e2e journeys` jobs are all green on the same head `4700fc10`, so the branch's 949 insertions across its 7 owned paths are not the red and no product work is outstanding. Two facts make it the eighth member rather than a repeat of the seventh: its review lock `agent/review/d086-count` has never existed on origin, so no session was ever handed the review that would clear the gate (unlike every earlier member, whose lock exists and merely cannot be re-issued), and the `scripts/nextTask.test.ts:47-62` first-pick pin that blocked D-273's park does not reach it — `D-086.count` is index 157 of the registry's 248 tasks and no test or script reads it — so the standard park at `owner` is available where D-273 (5) measured it unavailable for the registry's head row. ## D-144 (proposed - pending owner registration). Skippy's seeker chat never invents, never charges, and always goes through the normal ask
Proposed by the step-2 build from the delegated task brief and the founder's two steers ("help him get the referral 100%, help him make a 100% great referral taking the job requirements" and "help him generate a new resume if he agrees that he know the concepts"), both relayed by the coordinating agent. The owner has not yet registered this as a decision; the rules below are what the code enforces and what QA proves, and this entry becomes a decision only when the owner confirms it.

- **The chat creates asks through the same function as the ask page.** The "Send it" step calls `db.createCompanyReferralRequest` with the normal after-create work (referrer email/Slack, /requests, activity log, D-114 outbox); it never charges in the chat. There is no parallel path.
- **The cost in the chat is the live price.** It is computed with the quote route's own math (`assignJobLevel` + `creditsForLevel` + `getCreditSummary`), never hardcoded (D-132). If the seeker is short, the chat shows the shortfall and links /plans.
- **No referrer yet still means held, never free.** An uncovered ask from the chat stays at "waiting for company coverage" and spends credits at first visibility, exactly like the ask page (D-034, D-047).
- **The seeker writes or confirms the note, and only those words are sent.** Skippy may suggest and may flag weak or unsupported parts, but the seeker's confirmed text is what the ask carries; a note edited after confirmation is refused at send and must be re-confirmed.
- **Reading the resume with the AI model needs the seeker's yes first.** Without consent the chat still works; it just does not read the file.
- **Skippy never invents.** Every suggested note line must quote the resume text it stands on, and a line with no grounding is dropped (D-076). Skippy never promises a referral: it says the ask was sent, not that someone will refer.
- **A tailored resume is appended to, never rewritten, and only from confirmed skills.** The original file is kept. A skill enters the tailored copy only after the seeker confirms they know it; the seeker approves the final text before it is used.
- **The referrer sees no more than the normal flow shows.** Name, resume and note stay hidden until the referrer accepts (D-022).
- **Job pages and notes are data, never instructions.** Text pasted from a job post or resume is never treated as a command.
- **Scope and limits.** Ask-only (no payments, no account changes, no messages to referrers), 20 messages per person per day, off unless `SKIPPIE_SEEKER_ENABLED` is set, and then visible only to the test-seeker accounts in `SKIPPIE_SEEKER_USER_IDS` until proved (QA before any wider list).

## Scoped owner merge-policy amendment, Oct 23, 2026

Direct owner authorization supersedes repeated full-local-gate requirements solely because main advances. For docs/registry-only PRs such as #466, a conflict-free integration that introduces no runtime delta against current main may land after focused checks, a separate independent delta review and required GitHub CI; a fresh full local gate is not required solely for main movement. Runtime PRs use GitHub's supported merge or merge-queue mechanism with actual required CI on the merge result, not historical-head CI, without bypassing required checks. Conflicts require explicit resolution and renewed independent review; they do not qualify automatically. This changes no product rule, task ownership, D-035 founder hold, phone-file/history hold, production verification or other applicable safety gate.

**Superseded in part by D-200 (4) (owner, Oct 24; recorded by the Oct 26 audit):** Tier 2 and Tier 3 docs/registry-only PRs land on green required CI with no separate delta review; Tier 1 keeps one independent review by a model other than the builder's and fixers' (D-151).

## D-307 measured scope correction (2026-11-09)

The original D-307 row and registration history above are preserved. Its ban on every existing-test edit and requirement that all existing suites pass unchanged were contradictory to its required behavior. On main `228eed342393e1a6f345f1dabc296770f9dbf2b2`, `scripts/nextTaskArchitectReview.test.ts` case (g) explicitly expects no REVIEW and a CI-rerun message for the same passed, exact-tip gate-only-red, quiet-lock packet state. The old `PICKER-architect-review-route.md` R3 and acceptance (g) prescribe that old behavior. An independent scratch run reproduced 50/50 baseline and 49 pass/1 fail after only the bounded new routing behavior, failing solely that case. The scratch patch was reverted. Builder report `ff1db785` and status commit `fa888b96` report the same measured contradiction; no runtime source was committed.

Bounded technical correction under the existing coordination and doc-maintenance scope, not a new founder product ruling: `PICKER-packet-pass-red-recovery` may additionally edit only case (g), its title and its two expectations, in `scripts/nextTaskArchitectReview.test.ts`. Replace the old rerun-only expectation with an explicit normal REVIEW object bound to the architect work, ordinary review lock and packet flag, plus an assertion that this is not ARCHITECT/MAKE GREEN/FIX. Do not delete, skip or focus the case, reduce its assertion count or change any other case in that file. The new focused test file must retain strong negative controls and prove truthful blocked-report preservation. All other original suites and cases stay unchanged and must pass. The old packet's R3 and acceptance (g) are superseded only for passed, exact-tip completed gate-only-red packets whose review lock is quiet; active passed locks and all unrelated states retain the existing safety boundaries. The picker cannot distinguish the rejected post-review marker case from an older red run waiting for a rerun using a reviewed-sha body: current `markersOn` calls subject-only `parseMarker`. D-307 deliberately chooses qualified re-review after quiet for that bounded state, not a loader or review-gate change.

Runtime allowlist is now only `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`, new `scripts/nextTaskPacketPassRed.test.ts`, the exact existing case (g) just named, and the new packet's Status section. No loader, review gate, workflow, test-integrity, scope-check, product, auth or money change. No owns remains a manual allowlist plus independent-review obligation, never an automated scope pass. Existing writer/fixer model exclusions, active locks, dependencies, re-sync, exact-head verdicts, D-190 and required CI remain. This amendment neither clears e0df's truthful blocked report nor changes any real claim/verdict/marker. After this docs correction has separate exact-version review and required green CI and normally lands, the legitimate existing holder must reassess the supported normal stage transition; do not fabricate a green or withdrawal. Runtime consistency and every acceptance item remain unverified until actual implementation tests prove them.
